### FLOURNOY: symmetries and transformations
    - symmetry: "change something and it still looks the same"
    - transformations underlie the notion of symmetry
    - GROUPS are a mathematical structure that nicely capture the
      properties of transformations (VI Arnold says the same)
    - symmetries are good for simplifying calculations
    - symmetry gives rise to interactions in particle physics (?)
    - types of transformations: global, local, discrete,
      continuous, finite, infinte, compact, non-compact,
      internal, external (spacetime)
    - "compact" -> the parameter controlling the transformation
      is restricted / bounded e.g. angle of rotation from `0` to
      `2π`
    - SR: theory of GLOBAL transformations,
    - GR: theory of LOCAL transformations

    - in physics, we have TRANSFORMATIONS that we execute, and we
      also have things that are TRANSFORMED. ie we have rotations
      and we have velocities. And we rotate the velocities.

    - So the things that transformations act on are almost as
      important as the transformations themselves.
    - And these are called "representations of the group"
      (although sometimes "representation" can actually mean
      a representation of the transformation either... so
      a double-meaning for "representation")

    - can have "_faithful_" and "_degenerate_" representations of
      a group

    - rotation groups in Physis: a SCALAR is a quantity where
      I can _rotate_ all I want but _doesn't change_ (Just
      like Bala's fundamental Physics lecture!)

    - note: a _scalar_ constitutes the identity representation of
      a (rotation) group
        - $MY_IMAGES/physics/rotation-group-matrix-representation.png

    - sometimes transformations on objects can't be represented
      by matrices and we use something more sophisticated: index
      notation and Einstein summation convention

    - WOW: if you combine a representation with it's DUAL
      representation, then the combination is typically
      INVARIANT.
        - `ř^T ∙ r = invariant`
        - $MY_IMAGES/physics/rotation-group-dual-representation-invariant.png


    - WOW: how do you create a "dual" representation of your
      group element? Take a representation element `r` and apply
      a metric `g` on it. That gives me an element of the dual
      representation `ř`.
        - `ř = gr`

    - note: the metric will always be symmetric so `g^T = g`

    - note!!: in the case of 2D rotations, the metric `g` is
      actually the identity matrix `I`
        - $MY_IMAGES/physics/metric-is-identity-matrix.png


### laws of physics "the same"
    - how do we make laws "the same" in all reference frames?
    - we express the laws as tensors (ie `tensor = tensor`).
    - This is guaranteed to take the same form under any
      transformation (covariance)
    - or even deeper: what is the most fundamental level we can
      define the physics? (is it the equations of motion? or
      what?)
    - well if we take the most FUNDAMENTAL DEFINITION as the one
      that works in most cases, ie one that works in classical
      physics, relativistic physics, quantum physics, field
      physics, particle physics... then it's the ACTION.
    - note: Action is `S = ∫Ldt`
    - so we write an _action_ for our physical system
    - and if we take the action as our definition, then the
      _action_ should be _invariant_ under these transformations
    - so if the action doesnt change under the transformations,
      then the laws of physics aren't changing...
    - if you have an action, you can get equations of motion from
      it (e.g. Euler-Lagrange equations of motion). And the
      motion equations will be covariant

### separation principle
    - But Einstein goes further. He introduces the "separation
      principle": that the measurement in the first box cannot,
      or should not, influence the wave function in the second
      box.
    - He writes:
        > My way of thinking is now this: properly considered,
        > one cannot [refute the "YES"] if one does not make use
        > of a supplementary principle: the "separation
        > principle."
        > That is to say: "the second box, along with everything
        > to do with its contents, is independent of what happens
        > with the first box (separated partial systems)."
        > If one adheres to the separation principle,
        > then one thereby refutes the second ... point of view,
        > and only the [first] point of view remains, according
        > to which the above state description is an incomplete
        > description of reality, or of the real states.

    - Einstein's "separation principle" is more commonly called
      "locality." But his terminology is better since sometimes
      locality means the impossibility of communication with
      signals moving faster than the speed of light.
        - _This is quite a different matter_.
        - Jan 2019: now I understand this (subtle) point: Maudlin
          was saying that in CLASSICAL physics spatial separation
          does not necessarily imply causal separation. (Because
          in classical physics theres no limit on the propagation
          speed of physical processes)
          - But in SR/GR, with the LIGHT-CONE structure, in
            appropriate circumstances, if something is spatially
            separated then it's also causally separated (ie if
            outside the light cone).

    - The "separation principle" has nothing a-priori to do with
      relativity. In some of his later writing Einstein referred
      to violations of the separation principle as "spooky
      actions at a distance" and even complained that they did
      not fall off with distance like many such influences.
    - Remember that the classical description also has a sudden
      jump in probabilities when one of the boxes was opened. The
      difference is that the ball was always in one box or the
      other.
        - (April 2019: ie in classical physics there are hidden
          variables or reality, ie an actual ball actually is in
          a box)

    - Comment: when applying thermodynamics to the universe you
      must use non-extensive thermodynamics. Entropy is
      considered to be an extensive property, i.e., its value
      depends on the amount of material present.
        - Constantino Tsallis has proposed a nonextensive entropy
          (Tsallis entropy), which is a generalization of the
          traditional Boltzmann-Gibbs entropy.
        - The rationale behind the theory is that Gibbs-Boltzmann
          entropy leads to systems that have a strong dependence
          on initial conditions.
        - In reality most materials behave quite independently of
          initial conditions.
        - Nonextensive entropy leads to nonextensive statistical
          mechanics, whose typical functions are power laws,
          instead of the traditional exponentials.

### Nima: Scattering
    - probability: you have to make loads and loads of
      measurements to see a probability emerge
    - so QM: for total precision, you need to make infinitely
      many measurements with an infinitely large measurement
      apparatus
    - so there's inevitable imprecision with any measurement you
      make (and if the measuring apparatus is too big it'll
      collapse under gravity)
    - so for precise measurements, make measurements from
      apparatus infinitely far away and measure the scattering
      angles/amplitudes

### Peter Lewis: Quantum Sleeping Beauty
    - https://philpapers.org/rec/LEWQSB-2
    - The Sleeping Beauty paradox in both epistemology and the
      QM many-worlds interpretation raise problems concerning
      subjective probability assignments.
    - Furthermore, there are striking parallels between the two
      cases;
    - in both cases personal experience has a branching
      structure, and in both cases the agent loses themselves
      among the branches.
    - However, the treatment of probability is very different in
      the two cases, for no good reason that I can see.
    - Suppose, then, that we adopt the same treatment of
      probability in each case. Then the dominant 'thirder'
      solution to the Sleeping Beauty paradox becomes
      incompatible with the tenability of the many-worlds
      interpretation.

    - Extra: http://www.3ammagazine.com/3am/philosophy-quantum-mechanics-important-poached-eggs/

    - 3:AM: You think there's no good reason to treat probability
      differently when trying to understand the quantum sleeping
      beauty do you? What is this - why is probability treated
      differently and what happens when you don't? And are you an
      Everettian?

    - PLewis: No, I'm not an Everettian, mainly because I can't
      see a way of finding probabilities in a many-worlds theory.
        - If every outcome of a quantum measurement actually
          occurs, each in a different branch of reality, then
          probability must somehow be grounded in self-location:
          after a measurement, each of my successors can be
          uncertain about which branch they occupy, and
          probabilities can measure this uncertainty.
        - The point of my quantum Sleeping Beauty paper was to
          explore the analogy between the branch-location
          uncertainty in the quantum case and the temporal
          location uncertainty induced my memory loss in the
          Sleeping Beauty case.
        - My claim was that if you treat the two kinds of
          uncertainty in a parallel way, then an Everettian has
          to be a 'halfer' about sleeping beauty (the unpopular
          position!).
        - This particular argument has been successfully refuted
          (by Groisman, Hallakoun and Vaidman, Analysis 2013).
        - But I'm still not convinced that self-location
          uncertainty can generate the probabilities we need to
          do QM.

### Lee Smolin (Not even wrong)
    - I agree that the statement "Time is real" is misleading and
      regret it. A more precise claim is "time is fundamental",
      in the sense theres no deeper formulation of physical laws
      that doesnt involve the evolution of the present state or
      configuration, by the continual creation of novel events
      out of present events.
    - ie at no level is time emergent from a timeless formulation
      of fundamental physics. If the Wheeler-deWitt equation is
      correct this would not be the case.
    - I see my papers on the 'real ensemble' formulation and
      'relational hidden variables' to be modest attempts to
      explore new hypotheses to complete QM.
    - To answer Peter, these are inspired by developments in QG,
      particularly the hypotheses that if time is not emergent,
      _space may be emergent_ from a network of dynamically
      evolving relationships. (as in spin foam models, causal
      dynamical triangulations, causal set models)
    - But if space is emergent, so is _locality_ and hence so
      must _non-locality_ be. Hence we may aim to show the origin
      of Bell-non-locality from _disorderings of locality_ which
      follows from the emergent nature of locality.
    - This may be a way to realize the idea, which is currently
      popular, but was first stated by Penrose in his papers on
      spin networks, that the GEOMETRY OF SPACE AND QUANTUM
      ENTANGLEMENT MAY HAVE A COMMON ORIGIN.

    - The "Axis of Evil" is a name given to an anomaly in
      astronomical observations of the Cosmic Microwave

      and hence the location of Earth a greater significance than
      might be expected by chance. Most scientists believe the

      accessible.

### The Einstein-De Sitter Debate and Its Aftermath (Michel Janssen)

    ### Metric field - Intertia v Gravity
    - It was probably is part in response to De Sitter and Mie
      distinguishing between an inertial and a gravitational
      component of the metric field that Einstein in 1918 gave
      a new and more satisfactory formulation of his equivalence
      principle: that inertia and gravity are of the _exact same
      NATURE_. (what does "nature" mean here?)

    ### Machs Principle - DeSitter debate
    - In late March 1917, De Sitter sent Einstein a side-by-side
      comparison of the two models, which can be seen as a draft
      of the paper he published shortly afterward in the
      Proceedings of the Amsterdam Academy of Sciences ( De
      Sitter 1917a).
    - Einstein objected to De Sitter's solution because there was
      no matter producing the curvature of the space-time it
      described.
    - In his reply of March 24, 1917, Einstein wrote: "It would
      be unsatisfactory, in my opinion, if a world without matter
      were possible. Rather, the field should be fully determined
      by matter and not be able to exist without the latter ."
    - Einstein would later introduce the term "Mach's principle"
      for this requirement ( Einstein 1918a).

    ### metric field
    - The following analogy helps understand the notion of
      a metric field well enough to read this handout. Consider
      a map of the earth (which is essentially a way of
      coordinatizing the globe).
    - We cannot simply take distances on the map (the coordinate
      distances) to represent distances on the globe (the actual
      or proper distances).
    - Eg: a horizontal line segment of 2 inches on the map near
      the equator will correspond to a larger distance on the
      globe than a horizontal line segment of 2 inches on the map
      near the poles.
    - For every point on the map, we need to specify a set of
      numbers with which we have to multiply distances on the map
      in the vicinity of that point (coordinate distances) to
      convert them to actual distances (proper distances).
    - We need more than one number because the conversion for
      north-south distances will be different from the conversion
      for east-west distances.
    - The numbers in such a set are called the components of the
      metric at that point.
    - The metric field is the collection of all such sets of
      numbers for all points on the map. The same thing we do
      here with 2D space (representing the 2D curved surface of
      the earth on a 2D Euclidean plane together with
      a specification of the metric field to do all conversion
      from coordinate distances to proper distances) we can do
      with 4D space-time as well.
    - There will now also be a number (the temporal component of
      the metric) by which we have to multiply coordinate time
      differences to convert them to proper time differences.

### Underdetermination: Cosmology
    - Jeremy Butterfield is concerned with aspects of
      underdetermination in cosmology.

    - He argues that cosmology is different from other sciences
      in one regard: the possible data in cosmology is limited to
      that of Earth's _past light-cone_ ...

    - ...as opposed to observable events anywhere in spacetime...

    - ...and that underdetermination in cosmology is often about
      models, eg. solutions of GR, rather than of underlying
      theories.

    - This situation implies the underdetermination of
      cosmological models is endemic: Butterfield discusses
      theorems which affirm that in most general


### Thought experiments
    - distill inchoate empirical information - which Mach traced
      to memory and _instinct_. Each investigator is a microcosm,
      embodying laws that govern the universe. As Mach's disciple
      Albert Einstein summarised, "I am a little piece of
      nature".

    - Perception differs from theory by being cognitively
      impenetrable. Experiments exploit this independence.

    - So do thought experiments - often in a way that picks up
      where experiment leaves off. First Galileo observed that
      the pendulum bob recovers its original height when the
      pendulum swings from one side to the other.

    - He extended this law of equal heights to balls rolling down
      a `\_/`-shaped track. He then elongated one side of the
      U-shaped track: `\__________` . . . . Galileo then imagined
      the track extended infinitely.

    - Since the ball never regains its height, the ball continues
      on in a straight line forever. This is correct - and
      a wonderful anticipation of the Newton's 1st law of motion.

    - However, Galileo was still enough of Aristotelian to think
      that the ball must follow a circular path - like any other
      object in the heavens.

    - He did not believe his mind's eye. The thought experiment
      was right. The thought experimenter was wrong.

    - Normally by symmetry we mean taking a mirror image along
      some line, and an object which looks the same is symmetric
      ("mirror symmetry")
    - But symmetry can mean more: eg _rotational_ symmetry:
      rotate an object by `x` and it looks the same as before
    - or _translational_ symmetry.. (card suits whizzing by)
    - Mathematicians generalised the idea of symmetry:
        - ie. take some object, apply some translation to it and
          you can't tell the difference (in some sense!)
            - $MY_IMAGES/physics/symmetry.jpg
        - it might seem like they've taken a perfectly good word
          and generalised it into something meaningless, but
          abstract symmetries occur constantly in Maths:
            - Bijections
            - Isomorphisms
            - Homeomorphisms
            - Diffeomorphisms
            - Isometry
        - Indeed symmetry helped motivate Abstract Algebra!

    - Noether took a physical system: eg binary stars.
        - Then transformed it. e.g:
            - rotate by some angle `λ`
            - shift up or down by `λ`
            - or stretch all the distances by `λ`
    - We're interested if the system is the "same" in some sense.
      Noether decided it was interesting to check if the _total
      energy_ of the object stays the same.
    - so a system has "symmetry under a translation" if the total
      energy of the system objects doesn't change after the
      translation.
    - Thats the symmetry part of Noether, but now to conservation
    - we often solve physics problems easily using conservation
      laws (e.g. total momentum is conserved)
    - but Noether explains where this conservation comes from

### Vectors (Bala)
    - In other words, the vector quantities `F` and `A` must
      carry a _dictionary_ in their very meaning so that if you
      tell me how coordinate system `S'` is situated with respect
      to `S`,  and you give me `F=mA`, then I can predict `F'_x,
      F'_y, A'_x, A'_y` for you.

    - In other words, if you want the physical law to be
      unchanged in form, (form invariant under a coordinate
      transformation), you must express them in terms of
      quantities whose transformation law is known, is
      prescribed, once and for all.

    - Such quantities are called _covariant quantities_. This is
      an important principle and is at the root of modern
      physics.
    - Physical laws are expressed in covariant quantities, to
      remain _form invariant_.

### P.W. Anderson:
    - The main fallacy of the reductionist hypothesis is it does
      not actually imply a "constructionist" one:
        - The ability to reduce everything to simple fundamental
          laws doesn't imply the ability to start from those laws
          and reconstruct the universe.
    - In fact, the more particle physicists tell us about
      fundamental laws, the less relevance they seem to have to
      the real problems of the rest of science. The
      constructionist hypothesis breaks down when confronted with
      the twin difficulties of _scale_ and _complexity_.
    - It turns out the behavior of large and complex aggregates
      of elementary particles shouldnt be understood in terms of
      a simple extrapolation of the properties of a few
      particles.
    - Instead, at each level of complexity, entirely new
      properties appear, and the understanding of the new
      behaviors requires research which is as fundamental in its
      nature as any other and will show how the _whole_ becomes
      not only more than the sum, but very _different_ from the
      sum of the parts.
    - We expect to encounter facinating fundamental questions
      when fitting together less complicated pieces into a more
      complicated system and understanding the new types of
      behavior that result: quantitative differences become
      qualitative ones.

### Einstein companion
    - $MY_NOTES/physics/spacetime/einstein_companion.txt
    - However general covariance should not be conflated with the
      general relativity of MOTION, as Einstein eventually
      conceded. As Janssen argues, Einstein's search for general
      covariance was one of a number of failed attempts to
      establish the relativity of arbitrary motion.

    - Janssen also argues however, that Einstein's theory does
      implement a related principle: "the relativity of the
      gravitational field" (CPAE 7, Doc. 31, [p. 20]).

    - According to this principle, based on the local equivalence
      of gravity and acceleration, two observers in arbitrary
      motion with respect to one another can both maintain to be
      at rest if they agree to disagree on whether or not there
      is a gravitational field present.

    - Einstein's criterion for the presence or absence of
      a gravitational field (the vanishing or nonvanishing of the
      so-called Christoffel symbols) is different from the
      criterion used by relativists today (the vanishing or
      nonvanishing of the so-called Riemann tensor).

    - This modern criterion DOES NOT LEAVE ROOM FOR DISAGREEMENT
      between different observers about the presence of
      a gravitational field.

    - Both as he was developing general relativity and in
      commenting on it in later years, Einstein often claimed
      that his theory made all motion relative. One way to make
      sense of these pronouncements, which when taken literally
      are simply false, is to take him to be talking about the
      relativity of the gravitational field instead.

### Bernard - Immortal Fire
    - Bernard: "If light is _ponderable_ then it must be subject to
      acceleration I should think..." [YES it is, see Lanczos]

    - 'Ask Astronomer' answer: Matter such as stars warp the
      geometry of space-time so the "shortest path" between two
      points may not be a straight line. This is one reason that
      light may be affected by gravity.
        - However, light has no mass so it is not ACCELERATED by
          gravitational forces. (wrong? See next answer)
        - Even when light travels on a curved path it does so at
          the "speed of light"

    - Stackexchange answer: Light (does) undergo acceleration due
      to gravity. Look up gravitational lensing. But it's
      "speed" stays the same! Only it's "velocity" changes!

        - If a photon is passing by something heavy, it will be
          accelerated towards that object, changing its course
          but not its speed.

        - If a photon is going towards or away from something
          heavy, it can't properly accelerate by changing speed.
          I'm not a physicist, but I believe that it increases or
          decreases energy by changing its frequency.
            - In other words, things that you would expect to
              increase its speed will instead increase its
              frequency ('blue-shifting' it if it's visible
              light),
            - and what you would expect to decrease its speed
              will instead decrease its frequency ('red-shifting'
              if it's visible light).

### Speed of light: Conceptual Physics
    - Why does the speed of light stay the SAME in free space
      / vacuum
    - Because of conservation of energy.
    - If the strength of the electric component of the EM wave
      weakened, then the magnetic part would weaken, which would
      weaken the electric part etc etc
    - Likewise if the electric component got stronger, the
      magnetic part would in turn get stronger, resulting in
      a runaway infinite energy wave...
    - So no energy must be lost or gained in free space...

### Einstein to Jaffe
    - You consider the transition to special relativity as the
      most essential thought of relativity, not the transition to
      general relativity.

    - I consider the reverse to be correct. I see the most
      essential thing in the overcoming of the _intertial
      system_. A thing that acts upon all processes but undergoes
      no reaction.

    - This concept is in principle no better than that of the
      center of the universe in Aristotelian physics.

### The road to relativity
    - Read the whole foreword! 3 key points...
    - Einstein: Space-time doesn't claim existence on its own,
      but only as a structural quality of the field (!! later:
      what 'field'?)
    - Like other foundational papers, the 1915 paper is a nodal
      point, summing up the past and opening wide vistas for the
      future: Einstein (1916):
        > It appears quantum theory would have to modify not only
        > Maxwellian electrodynamics but also the new theory of
        > gravitation
    - However there is hope:
        - GR and special-relativistic quantum field theories
          share one fundamental feature not often stressed:
            - the primacy of PROCESSES over states.
    - The 4D approach, emphasising process in regions of
      space-time is basic to both.
        - The ideal approach to QG is a background-independent
          method of quantisation that takes PROCESS as primary
          (like Whitehead!)

### Road to Relativity - Einsteins Odyssey
    - In the Prague papers, he focused on developing a theory of
      the static gravitational field based on the equivalence
      principle. Just like Newtons theory, it involved
      a gravitational potential represented by a single scalar
      function, now given by a _variable speed_ of light. (?)

    - Nevertheless some basic features of GR had already been
      conceived. Among them the idea that the source of the
      gravitational potential is not just the mass of concrete
      bodies but also the equavalent mass of the energy of the
      gravitational field itself.

    - However until the end of the Prague period, Einstein still
      assumed that the gravitational potential was represented by
      a single function:
        - the space-dependent speed of light.
        - Also his theory was still restricted to a static
          gravitational field.

    - ... however the decisive idea of the analogy between the
      mathematical formulation of GR and the Gaussian theory of
      _surfaces_ only came to me in 1912...

### Darrigol
    - ... rule:  parallelogram of forces, which Newton derived
      from his 2nd law. Remember in his derivation, the second
      impulse comes _right after_ the first and increases the
      momentum vector by a specific amount.

    - Huygens and Euler regarded force as a convenient way to
      represent mechanical actions without describing their true
      _contact-driven_ cause.

    - Galileo, Newton, Carnot and Saint-Venant were all
      contingentists. Galileo was attacking the dogmatism of
      inherited scholasticism, Newton and Saint-Venant were
      denouncing the rationalists pretense of reading the
      Creators mind and Carnot was expressing the engineers
      common sense. Yet the declared contingentists occasionally
      flirted with rationalism.

    - Cartesian rationalism followed the Cartesian worldview to
      the grave. With the partial exceptions of Kant and
      Helmholtz, the following generations of natural
      philosophers professed the empirical origin of any
      knowledge of nature and denounced the arrogance of a mind
      that would derive the laws of nature by mere
      _introspection_.

### Jobs for Physicists (HN)
    - The ubiquitous lack of secure longer term jobs like
      Einstein's civil service job at the patent office -- he was
      not a post-doc -- make deeper conceptual analysis of the
      outstanding problems in physics today difficult, probably
      impossible.

    - truly don't think Einstein's work at the patent office was
      "low hanging fruit." -- quite the opposite. Most major
      scientific breakthroughs have required at least five years
      of work, usually frustrating trial and error, failures,
      blind alleys etc. A post-doc with a two year appointment,
      which is typical, simply doesnt have the time. He or she
      needs to publish something "impressive" in that two years.
      Einstein was in the enviable position of not having to
      worry about his next post-doc or whatever from 1903
      onwards. In addition his patent work involved electrical
      and electromagnetic inventions that dovetailed well with
      his interests and background.

    - Modern physics has very few long term secure jobs to think
      about actual physics. Tenured faculty spend most of their
      time writing grant applications and fund raising -- not the
      same thing.

### Frame dragging (gyroscopes)
    - ./spacetime/gyroscope-mystery-inertia.md
    - That is the question: At what, exactly, is the gyroscope pointed?

    - While still formulating GR, Einstein calculated that the
      gravitational field of a shell of matter rotating around
      a gyroscope (picture a gyro inside a spinning, hollow
      Earth) would displace the gyro's axis relative to the fixed
      stars.

    - The gravitational field literally drags around the gyro's
      "inertial compass."

    - In 1918, Austrian physicist Hans Thirring, with Einstein's
      input, published a similar calculation based on the
      completed relativity theory.

    - Josef Lense, an Austrian mathematician, later provided
      relevant astronomical observations. Inevitably, this
      concept of _frame dragging_ has come to be known as the
      Lense-Thirring effect.


### Faraday: Experimental Researches (Oct 2017)
    - I have long held an opinion, almost amounting to
      a conviction, in common I believe with many other lovers of
      natural knowledge, that the various forms under which the
      forces of matter are made manifest have one common origin;

    - ...in other words, are so direclty related and mutually
      dependent, that they are convertible, as it were, one into
      another, and posses equivalents of power in their action.

    - This strong persuasion extended to the powers of light and
      led to many exertions, having for their object the
      discovery of the direct relation of _light_ and
      _electricity_, and their mutual action in bodies subject
      jointly to their power.

    - but the results were negative and were afterwards
      confirmed, in that respect, by Wartmann.

    - these ineffectual exertions, and many others which were
      never published, could not remove my _strong persuasion_
      derived from philosophical considerations; and therefore
      I recently resumed the inquiry by experiement in a most
      strict and searching manner and have at last succeeded:

        > and in illuminating a magnetic line of force

### Einstein: coordinates have no meaning(?)
    - Thus when writing the Perihelion paper Einstein was still
      influenced by Hilbert's electromagnetic theory of matter.

    - On the other hand, Einstein was already departing from Hilbert's
      theory of matter in his November 18 work, because he wrote,
      "through which time and space are deprived of the last trace of
      objective reality".

    - Hence, Einstein began to realize that space and time coordinates
      have no meaning in general relativity.

### Motl on Maudlin
    - Maudlin is: Another math hater. He believes the maths used
      by physicists isn't capable of attaching a directionality
      (sign) to timelike intervals in the spacetime.

    - Be aware that it is possible to equip `spacetime` with this
      structure because the set of nonzero timelike vectors isn't
      connected (it is composed of the future light cone and the
      past light cone) and physicists are fully aware of this
      trivial fact.

### inertia
    - An object at rest tends to stay at rest. An object in motion
      tends to stay in motion.

    - Newton dismantled the traditional idea that objects would
      _slow down over time_, and described the concept of
      inertia: the amount objects resist changes in their motion.

      you will always have the advantage."

### An anomaly in space and time and the origin of dynamics
    http://arxiv.org/abs/1605.01965

    Hamiltonian defines the dynamical properties of the
    universe. Evidence from particle physics shows that
    there is a different version of the Hamiltonian for each
    direction of time. As there is no physical basis for the
    universe to be asymmetric in time, both versions must
    operate equally. However, conventional physical theories
    accommodate only one version of the Hamiltonian and one
    direction of time. This represents an unexplained
    anomaly in conventional physics and calls for
    a reworking of the concepts of time and space. Here
    I explain how the anomaly can be resolved by allowing
    dynamics to emerge phenomenologically. The resolution
    offers a picture of time and space that lies below our
    everyday experience, and one in which their differences
    are epiphenomenal rather than elemental.

### Maudlin: Space-like separated events are unusual
    - Spatial separation would be a perfect insulator from causal
      influence if no influence could go faster than light.
    - Since Aspect's experiment shows no such insulating effects,
      some direct causal connections must exist between
      space-like separated events.
    - But we might now wonder why this result should provoke any
      anxiety about conflicts with Relativity. For in deriving
      the Lorentz transformation and explicating the relativistic
      space-time structure we nowhere assumed that the speed of
      light acts as any sort of a limit on anything. We used only
      the invariance of the speed of light as encapsulated in the
      Law of Light.
    - Nonetheless, Relativity is commonly taken to imply some
      sort of restriction on how fast something can go. And given
      the _fundamental difference_ between space-like and
      time-like separated events, one might suspect that
      connections between space-like separated events may display
      rather _peculiar_ and counterintuitive properties.
    - Our job and in the following three chapters, is to explore
      what sort of limit the speed of light might be. On the
      quantum non-locality side, we also want to determine what
      sort of limit the speed of light cannot be


### Maudlin: 2 facts about the speed of light
    The invariance of the speed of light in a vacuum
    coprises _two_ equally important facts:

    1. The speed of a light ray must be unaffected by the
       state of motion of its _source_. This is not typical
       behavior for any sort of projectile. The independence
       of light trajectories from the state of light sources
       is a very surprising fact. It implies there must be
       some objective structure which the light is tied
       into, a structure which determines its path.

       No such structure exists in Galilean space-time per
       se. One can add extra structure by introducing
       material bodies into the space-time; for example the
       rest frame of a body of water is the unique Galilean
       frame in which water waves travel the same speed in
       all directions.

       But the Special Theory rejects the notion that light
       is a disturbance of some material medium. The
       structure which determines light trajectories is
       built into space-time itself.

    2. Even more remarkable is the 2nd consequence of the
       Law of Light. Not only must the speed of light be
       independent of the state of its source, it must be
       independent of the inertial frame in which the speed
       is measured!

### vectors and bases (May 2017)
    This always confused me a bit at Lboro but I see it
    today.

    Firstly we can say that a vector `|a>` is _represented_
    by a column matrix (where the column is a set of
    components for an agreed choice of basis):

    ```
    |a>   is represented by   [a_1]
                              [a_2]
    ```

    We say "represented by", because the actual vector `|a>`
    has the same meaning in all bases, while a specific
    column vector represents the vector only in a specific
    basis. But... when the choice of basis is fixed, theres
    no harm in linking the two concepts with an equals sign.
    So for an 2D space we can write:

    ```
    |a>     =   [a_1]
                [a_2]
    ```

    But heres the real kicker. If you choose basis vectors
    with these components:
    ```
    |e_1>   =   [1]       |e_2>   =   [0]
                [0]                   [1]
    ```
    Everything is good in this representation, becuase the
    column matrices obey rules appropriate for
    vectors. So we have:

    ```
    | a >   =   a_1 |e_1>  +   a_2 |e_2>

            =   a_1 [1]    +   a_2 [0]
                    [0]            [1]

            =       [a_1]  +     [ 0 ]
                    [ 0 ]        [a_2]

            =       [a_1]
                    [a_2]
    ```
    So really, the two basis vectors `|e_1>` and `|e_2>` are
    lurking invisible behind `[a_1]` all the time...
                             `[a_2]`


### Maudlin twins
    - Twin _A_: Jets "off" (on 2 sides of the triangle)
    - Twin _B_: Stays at "home" (on 1 side of the triangle)

    For Maudlin, its just that Twin A travels a _longer_
    spacetime route, and because of the anti-triangle rule,
    longer spacetime means a _shorter_ elapsed time.

    He says its _not_ because of acceleration per se. For
    example, he adds equal acceleration for Twin B, but just
    does it for a little triangle "bump". And the result is
    Twin B is _still_ much older, even with this equal
    accleleration bump.

    My thought: So its not acceleration, but how "long"
    someone has accelerated off and _stayed on_ another
    inertial path. Regarding the little acceleration "bump"
    for Twin B - alright he has the same "acceleration" now
    as twin A, but he only stays on the "new" inertial path
    for a short time before accelerating back to the
    original inertial path (straight side of triangle).
    So... acceleration _is_ a culprit?, but not literally
    the _amount_ of acceleration.

    - confusion 1: all motion is relative, so Twin A should
      equal Twin B
    - confusion 2: acceleration explains it
    - confusion 3: fast clock dilate

### angular momentum
    - (A Rudimentary History of Dynamics - Andrew Ross)
    - The law of angular momentum is commonly treated as
      a consequence of linear momentum, but that is no more true
      than the common statement that Newton's First Law can be
      ascertained from his Second.
    - This approach works for special cases only: it is not true
      in general. The Newtonian equations cannot contemplate
      deformable bodies or motion of a continuum without severe
      restriction. Angular momentum is a law of me- chanics
      independent from any other. It took most of the 18th
      century for this to be realised.

### Plancks constant
    - `h` -> plancks const is `J•s`... NOT `J•(s^-1)`
    - Plancks constant has dimensions of _physical action_:
        - energy x time,
        - or also: momentum x distance
        -         (momentum x length)
    - In SI units, it's expressed in:
        - `J⋅s` joule seconds
        - `N⋅m⋅s`
        - `kg⋅m^2⋅s^−1`
        - `kg⋅m^2/s` <--- units of angular momentum!
        - note: 1 Newton = kg⋅m⋅s^−2
    - `h-bar, ħ` has units of angular momentum (Flournoy)
    - `h-bar, ħ` = "reduced Planck constant"
    - A modified form of Planck’s constant called h-bar (ℏ), or
      the reduced Planck’s constant, in which ℏ equals h divided
      by 2π, is the quantization of _angular momentum_.
    - Eg: The angular momentum of an electron bound to an atomic
      nucleus is quantized and can only be a multiple of `h-bar`.
    - GPT discussion:
        - The units "Joule seconds" (J·s) and "kilogram meter
          squared per second" (kg·m^2/s) are not the same, but
          are closely related through dimensional analysis.

            1. Joule (J):
                - A Joule is a unit of energy.
                - By definition, 1 Joule = 1 Nm = 1 kg·m^2/s^2.

            2. Planck's Constant (h):
                - The units of Planck's constant are Joule
                  seconds (J·s).

        - To see how they relate, we break down the units
          step-by-step:

            1. Energy in Joules:
                - 1 Joule (J) = 1 kg·m^2/s^2.

            2. Multiplying by Time:
                - When you multiply energy (J) by time (s), you
                  get:
                - J·s = (kg·m^2/s^2)·s = kg·m^2/s.

        So, _Joule seconds_ (J·s) simplifies to _kilogram meter
        squared per second_ (kg·m^2/s) when you perform the
        dimensional analysis:
            - J·s = (kg·m^2/s^2)·s = kg·m^2/s.

        - This shows the units of Planck's constant (J·s) are
          equivalent to kg·m^2/s in terms of dimensional
          analysis, but the interpretations are different:

        - _Joule seconds_ (J·s) emphasizes the product of energy
          and time, which is significant in contexts such as
          'action' in physics.

        - _Kilogram meter squared per second_ (kg·m^2/s) focuses
          on the combination of mass, distance, and time, which
          appears in various mechanical contexts. (angular
          momentum)

        - In summary, while "J·s" and "kg·m^2/s" are
          dimensionally equivalent, they emphasize different
          physical concepts.



### Angular momentum
    - https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angular_momentum
    - Note Terminology:
        - angular momentum
            - "moment of momentum"
            - "rotational momentum"
        - moment of interia
            - "angular mass"
            - "rotational inertia"
            - "mass moment of inertia"
    - Just like angular velocity, there are two special types of
      angular momentum:
        - Spin angular momentum:
            - defined as the angular momentum of an object about
              its centre of mass coordinate.
        - Orbital angular momentum:
            - defined as the angular momentum of the centre of
              mass of an object about a chosen _origin_.
    - The total angular momentum of an object is the sum of the
      spin and orbital angular momenta.
    - Angular momentum can be considered a rotational analog of
      linear momentum. Thus, where linear momentum `p` is
        - `p = m v`
        - angular momentum  `L` is proportional to moment of
          inertia `I` and angular speed `ω`
        - `L = I   ω`
        - `ℓ = r^2 Ω,` <--- Fragile living review
            -  units `I`:
                - for a point mass, the moment of inertia is
                  simply the mass times the square of the
                  perpendicular distance to the axis of rotation
                - `kg⋅m^2`
            -  units `ω`:
                - `1/s, s^−1` (radians per second)
            - units angular momentum!
                - `kg⋅m^2⋅s^−1`
    - Unlike mass, which depends only on amount of matter, moment
      of inertia is also dependent on the position of the axis of
      rotation and the shape of the matter.
    - Unlike linear velocity, which does not depend upon the
      choice of origin, orbital angular velocity is always
      measured with respect to a fixed origin. Therefore, `L`
      should be referred to as the angular momentum relative to
      that center (strictly speaking).
     - Ratio 1: Moment of inertia:
        - `I` is defined as the RATIO of the net angular
          momentum `L` of a system to its angular velocity `ω`
          around a principal axis.
            - `I = L /  ω`
            - `L = I  ω`
            - `p = m v` analog
     - Ratio 2: Moment of inertia:
        - If the shape of the body does not change, then its
          moment of inertia appears in Newton's law of motion as
          the RATIO of an applied torque `τ` on a body to the
          angular acceleration `α` around a principal axis:
        - `I = τ / α`
        - `τ = I α`
        - `F = m a` analog

### LJMU dynamics
    1. Orbital Speed Equation
    2. Acceleration Equation
    3. Orbital Period Equation

    # Note:
    None of these three equations has the variable
    `M_{satellite}` in them.

    The _period_, _speed_ and _acceleration_ of a satellite
    are only dependent on:
    - the radius of orbit
    - the mass of the central body the satellite is orbiting

    Just like  projectile motion on earth, the _mass of the
    projectile_ has no effect on the acceleration towards
    the earth and the speed at any instant. When air
    resistance is negligible and only gravity is present,
    the _mass of the moving object becomes a non-factor_.
    Such is the case of orbiting satellites.

### Weinberg chat (closer to truth)
    - Often beautiful mathematics pre-exists physics and
      turns out to be extraordinarily useful.
    - Example: the theory of symmetry. The theory is called
      "group theory" which is the mathematical underpinning
      of _physical principles_ of symmetry.
    - Symmetry principles describe things that don't change
      their appearance when _you_ change your point of view.
    - And the set of all ways of changing your point of view
      which leaves something _unchanged_ is called the
      group. The "symmetry group".


### Black bodies
    - $MY_NOTES/physics/black-body.txt
    - Our difficulty is we are used to thinking that black
      objects are also _dark_(they do not SHINE)
    - This is because in everyday life, bodies either absorb or
      reflect light, and their color is determined by what
      wavelengths they _reflect_.
        - If objects are black (absorbing) theyre also dark (send
          no light back to us).
    - However, bodies can also _emit_ light all by themselves,
      and if hot enough, we will see this emission.
    - The burner of an electric stove starts out black, but when
      heated it glows red.
    - For a physicist, its still a black body: the black covering
      still absorbs any light hitting it. But in addition, it
      _GLOWS_. Stars are the same.

### potential (difference)
    - Even "potential" without the word "difference" is really
      a potential difference, because then the reference is taken
      either at infinity or a ground.
    - The potential at a point is the work per unit charge that
      is done to move a positive test charge from the reference
      point to the given point.
    - The potential difference between point `A` and `B` is:
        - the potential difference between point `A` and the
          reference point
        - minus
        - the potential difference between point `B` and the
          reference point

### check later
    - It may be worth mentioning that Electric Potential (or
      gravitational) is, strictly, defined as the energy needed
      to take a unit charge (or unit mass, in the other case)
      from infinity to the point you are dealing with. The reason
      for taking infinity as your reference point is that you
      have to choose somewhere and if you choose zero distance,
      it introduces problems when you do `1/r1 - 1/r2`.
    - You can have an object at any potential but you can't use
      the energy unless the charge / mass goes to another
      potential.

### voltage 1
        ```
       *          *   <--- push (electrical work)
       e1         e2

       *       *   <--- push
       e1      e2

       *   *  <--- push
       e1  e2
        ```

      - Now potential is stored in the second electron `e2`.
      - When you release `e2`, it will flow, and that is current.
      - Hence (??) voltage is the work done (or available to?)
        _per unit charge_.
      - Hence (!!) if you have one coulomb of charge, and you do
        one joule of work on it, you get one volt.

### voltage 2
    - The difference in electric potential energy at two points
      in a circuit is what we call the voltage.

### voltage 3
    - Potential difference, or voltage, is the _difference_ in
      electric _potential energy_ between _two points_.
    - It is denoted by `∆V` and has units of volts, or joules per
      Coulomb.

### capacitors
    Pretty much like a _battery_ except you can charge them
    and discharge them ad-infinitum. Also they drain/fade
    after a few seconds/minutes.

    To charge a capacitor you connect both ends to
    a battery. Then remove the connection and the plates are
    left in "tension". When you reconnect the plates again,
    charge will flow, and you can use this flow to do
    things: eg. light a bulb.

    Area of research: build an "ultra capacitor"

### Thomas K Simpson: The Scientist as Gardener
    Bacon, in his essay on Gardens, reminds us that "In the
    beginning, God Almighty first planted a garden," adding
    that, "Indeed, it is the rarest of pleasures"; and we
    have seen that Faraday too, delights in the works of
    the Creation.

    It is a nice suggestion to think of the Natural Order as
    a garden, and to imagine Faraday as God's Gardener.
    Faraday has such a sense of the life of his materials,
    and we can think of the Diary as capturing the special
    wisdom of a gardener's notebook. Bacon's voice is surely
    the opposite of Faraday's. Bacon is the Lord Chancellor,
    thinking of God as giving directions and having His
    staff do the digging. By contrast Faraday is the hard
    worker - loving, knowing and managing the earth and all
    its creatures.

    The magnetic pattern is not only perfect, it is
    fundamentally beautiful. To know it is to know it as
    beautiful. To make its pattern is to cultivate lovingly,
    rather than to construct. The scientist's reward is _not
    as creator, but as cultivator or gardener_. The
    scientist is one who opens us to the truth and beauty of
    a work which _belongs first to another_. It is a role of
    humility.

    Faraday's mind is constantly inventive and busy - but it
    is always the wise and understanding mind of the
    master-gardener. It is never the role of the gardener to
    explain or create life itself. Euclid knows his figures
    thoroughly because they are the works of mind not unlike
    his own. Faraday knows the wondrous magnetic patterns as
    _works of a Creator far greater than himself_. Yet this
    garden is our inheritance, and Faraday is taking full
    charge on behalf of us all!

### natural units
    All we talk about in physics are dimensionless
    quantities. There is nothing fundamental about any
    units.

    When we say something is 2m hight, what are we really
    saying? We're comparing a bar in Paris to whatever we
    are measuring. ie we are talking about a _ratio_!

    "Natural Units" are much better. Everything can be
    reduced to a length or an energy or a mass. ie. the
    units have physical significance. eg. my height in
    Hydrogen atoms.

### Miller
    * magnetism is almost entirely based on QM spin.
    * anything that is moving or changing in time is in
      a superposition state.
    * the idea of superposition is already well known in
      classical physics. Many waves in classical physics show
      linear superposition (eg light and radio waves) Waves can
      pass right through each other, and waves on the sea surface
      can be thought of as a superposition of waves going in
      different directions.
    * but we weren't expecting that matter could be thought of as
      a superposition. However it appears that on the atomic
      level this idea of superposition works.
    * not all classical fields are conservative (irrotational).
      Eg. Vortex fields (like a draining bathtub) do not have the
      property that the change in potential energy around any
      closed path is zero. If you go round the path against the
      vortex you may expend more energy that if you had gone
      around in the other direction.
        - (So your energy depends on past history, whereas true
          potential energy only depends on position and not past
          history). Some effects associated with magnetic fields
          are not conservative.
    * when we write the total energy as the sum of the potential
      (position) and kinetic energies(momentum) this can be
      called the "Hamiltonian"
    * in QM we're more inclined to use the gradient of
      a potential rather than the idea of a force directly.
    * technically `ω` (omega) is the number of _radians_ per
      second rather than the number of cycles per second

### normal modes
    - For the coupled pendula, can we think of a way in which the
      system can move in a _steady state_, in which each mass
      oscillates with _CONSTANT amplitude_?

    - We will call this way of moving a _normal mode_. In fact,
      we need to think of two normal modes, since we know that
      we'll need two second-order DEQs to describe the system.
      (After all, we started with two DEQs, equations 5.2.1a and
      b.)

    - The 'breathing' mode and the 'pendulum' mode are the two
      normal modes of the coupled pendulum system. We will see
      that we can find similar normal modes even for very
      complicated systems of many masses interacting through many
      different forces.

    - All these normal modes share the characteristics of the
      breathing and pendulum modes shown above, leading us to the
      definition:

    - _Definition of a normal mode_: A way in which the system
      can move in a steady state, in which all parts of the
      system move with the same _frequency_. The parts may have
      different (perhaps zero or negative) amplitudes.

### Learnt this week
    - Lange: how to visualise Maxwell's incompressible fluid as
      a way of representing the EM field.
    - Fluid speed is analogous to electric force. Speed is
      inversely proportional to `1/r^2` so the fluid flows
      _slower_ the further it is from the charge.
    - Because its slower this means the force exerted by the
      charge is weaker (by analogy)

    - Why actually does the fluid flow slower as you get further
      out?
        - Well the flow _rate_ stays the same!! Thats what
          incompressible means. So if you have something flowing
          out of a greater area, the actual speed its moving at
          must slow down if you want the total flow _rate_ to
          stay the same.

### lange
    One final point. the electromagnetic field, in virtue of
    its mass, possess a _gravitational_ influence (according
    to GR), and so affects the motion even of uncharged
    bodies (Feynmann et al. 1963: II, p. 27-6)

    So an incredibly sensitive experiment could tell us the
    mass distribution in a region containing an
    electromagnetic field. It would also tell whether it is
    correct to assign the zero level of potential energy
    ("sea-level") to the condition where the field is zero,
    since the absolute _amount_ of mass, not mere mass
    differences, would affect the gravitational force felt
    by a test body.

    This is a nice example of why its problematic to say
    that 2 interpretations of a theory are "empirically
    equivalent" - that is, they make all the same observable
    predictions.

    Although 2 different assignments of energy's zero level
    yield the same observable predictions in classical
    physics (where only energy _differences_ matter), they
    lead to different observable predictions when
    _combined_ with GR.

    No two interpretations of a theory are empirically
    equivalent with _all possible_ hypotheses that might be
    _added_ to them.

### Lange: Energy / Momentum
    - Momentum and energy are sibling concepts.
        - both arise from the _accumulation of force_.
            - momentum: accumulation over TIME
            - energy:   accumulation over SPACE

    * momentum:
        - accumulation of force over _time_
        - see Barbour section below where I multiply a small `f`
          by a `δt` to get a change in momentum.
        - (but deBroglie   `p = h/λ`)

    * energy:
        - accumulation of force over _space_
        - (but Planck      `E = hf`)

    - This relation between momentum and energy will become
      important in relativity theory ...
        - which breaks the distinction between time and space,
        - and hence between momentum and energy,
        - thereby unifying them.

### why cant we feel the earth rotating 1.
    - The real answer is we _can_ feel turning, just that the
      earth is turning so slowly that it is below our human
      perception limit.

    - Because the rotation of the earth is very smooth and
      doesn't change, the centripetal acceleration _we feel_ is
      very nearly constant.
    - This means that the (small) _centrifugal_ force from the
      rotation gets added to gravity to make up the "_background_
      force" we don't notice.

### why cant we feel the earth rotating 2.
    - ... there is a further deep reason why dynamics took so
      long to discover. In accordance with Newton's laws, the
      earth is in a state of rotation.
    - Nevertheless, a frame of reference fixed to the _surface of
      the earth approximates a state of uniform motion_ to a very
      good degree,
    - and since according to Newton, a state of uniform motion is
      indistinguishable from one of rest, the Galilean invariance
      of dynamics produces a powerful impression that the earth
      is immobile.

### Guage
    - Gauge transformations in physical theories can lead to
      possibly unpalatable conclusions if you believe a certain
      way.

    - This sounds very interesting. Could you provide some
      examples?

    - Basically lots of physical observables in nature are
      _derivatives_ of things. So if the underlying thing
      contains a _constant term_, it goes away.
        - Because it goes away, you are free to choose it however
          you like and still get the same observable result.

    - So the question then becomes how real you believe those
      underlying objects are. The linked article creates a setup
      to challenge that.

### potential energy
    - It is worth noting that the modern word 'potential', as in
      "potential energy", was deliberately introduced in the
      Aristotelian sense (existing in a latent or undeveloped
      state).

    - In modern science, _vis potentialis_ was first used in 1750
      by the Bernoullis and Euler. The concept of _potential
      function_, with many uses in mathematics and physics, was
      introduced by Green in 1828.

    - Finally, the expression _potential energy_ was introduced
      in 1853 by Rankine in explicit contrast to _actual energy_.
        - Actual energy was renamed kinetic energy by Thomson and
          Tait, which obscured Rankine's Aristotelian contrast of
          potential and actual.

    The soundness of Mach's critique can be seen from the
    following analogy.

    Imagine a people constructing a mechanics who are
    confined to a tiny part of the earth's surface and cant
    see the stars. They will ascribe special physical
    attributes to the VERTICAL DIMENSION of space (direction
    of the acceleration of falling bodies) and on the
    grounds of such a conceptual basis, will offer reasons
    why the earth is in most places horizontal.

    They might not be influenced by the argument that the
    geometrical properties of space are isotropic and that
    it is unsatisfactory to postulate physical laws with
    a preferred direction; (the vertical direction)

    - They will be inclined (analogously to Newton) to assert the
      absoluteness of the vertical as something proved by
      experience and one simply has to come to terms with it.

    - The preference of the vertical over all other spatial
      directions is precisely analogous to the preference given
      to _inertial systems_ over other rigid co-ordination
      systems.

    - (but! There was a _physical reason_ behind the seeming
      difference of the vertical DIRECTION for the confined
      people. Maybe there is a physical reason too for the

### thoughts on change in momentum
    - Really you have a change in the _velocity_ vector due to
      a _force_, but the visible measurable outcome of the force
      will depend on the _mass_ of the body. And momentum
      captures this effect of mass. The amount of mass coupled to
      whatever the change in velocity is, gives the 'oomph' in
      momentum.

    - By defining the momentum concept you can keep the laws more
      abstract and generic, because the actual effect of any give
      force on a body's velocity will depend on the body's mass.

    - ∆v = (something) + huge mass   -->  small momentum change
    - ∆v = (something) + small mass  -->  big momentum change

    - So mass kind of "couples" with the velocity change to
      produce the momentum change.

### 'change' versus 'rate'
    Note: theres a big difference between:
    - an (actual) change in momentum (∆M)
    - and a rate of change in momentum (dM/dt)

    `rate    =  f   = dM/dt`
    `change  =  ∆M  = (dM/dt) δt`

    - the rate is just a potential change, but needs to combine
      (couple?) to a time period to produce an actual change.

    - modern terminology:
        - force is the _rate_ of change of momentum.
        - not!!! the (actual) change in momentum.

### impulse
    - is a force _f_ acting for time _δt_,
        - in the limit where _δt_-> 0

    - i.e. the force only acts for an infinitesimal time
     - `F = f * (δt)`

     where
     _f_ is a continuous "force" (_f_= dM/dt)
     _F_ is the "force" produced by a collision
            which in modern terms is actually called an "impulse"

    - Deep question: why do we see _scales_?
    - Another question:  why is the universe so big?
    - This is one of the deepest mysteries of physics that
      goes by the name the 'cosmological constant'.

    - Now if you do an experiment in one galaxy cluster, and an
      experiment in another cluster, they don't interfere with
      each other. This fact is called '_locality_' - or as I like
      to call it '_cluster decomposition_'. (pun on the cluster
      decomposition theorem in QFT.)

    - If you take the QFT framework and impose 'locality' in the
      sense of 'cluster decomposition' then you find the
      mathematics takes you step-by-step to a unique conclusion:

        > different SCALES will DECOUPLE from each another

    - Now previous speakers have said the _Universe_ being
      exceptionally simple is a gift. This is also a gift. For
      example, if Matthew had to describle what was happening at
      the Planck scale in order to do condensed matter physics
      - that would be impossible.

    - So we have been given a gift; we can separate things into
      a scale, but to me the amazing thing is it follows from
      a _mathematical construction_.

    - So the math is so powerful it allows us to derive this from
      the basic principle of 'cluster decomposition'.

    - Now we know we have to give up locality(!). As we look at
      smaller and smaller objects, the energy of the microscope
      light gets larger and larger until it is so large it
      creates a black hole(!). So we cannot go any deeper than
      the event horizon of the BH.

    - And the same at large distances, the acceleration of the
      universe poses a limit.

### The foundation of physics! (McCauley)
    - For horizontal motions, Galileo contradicted Aristotle and
      discovered the fundamental idea of _inertia_ (without
      naming it so)

    - extrapolating from experiments where the friction of an
      inclined plane is systematically reduced, he asserted that
      a body, once set into motion, tends to continue moving at
      constant velocity on an ideal frictionless horizontal plane
      without any need to be pushed along continually:

        `g_y = constant `
            - (Mar 2022: ??? this eqn is for gravity, not for
              a horizontal plane?)

    - These ideas had occurred in more primitive forms earlier.
      It was only a short step further for Decartes to assert
      that the _natural_ state of motion of an undisturbed body
      is _neither absolute rest nor uniform circular motion_, but
      rectilinear motion at constant speed.

    - --------------------------------------
    - This law is the foundation of physics!
    - --------------------------------------

    - Without the law of inertia, Newton's second law would not
      follow and there'd be no physics as we know it in the sense
      of universal laws of motion and force...

    - Galileo believed rectilinear motion at constant speed to be
      only an initial _transient_. He expected that the orbit of
      a hypothetical projectile tossed far from the earth must
      eventually merge into a circle in order to be consistent
      with his belief in a finite and perfectly spherical
      universe.

    - He was therefore able to believe that planets and stars
      could move in circles about the sun as a result of _inertia
      alone_. He saw no need for gravity.
        - (Ah this explains references I saw to Galileos
          "circular inertia")

    - BUT!
    - they both HIT THE GROUND at the SAME TIME!!!
    - I was puzzled at first, but then realised:
    - thats just the _superposition_ of 2 motions
    - both balls have the same force acting in the y direction:
      i.e. gravity! (which controls when they hit the ground)
    - So it doesnt matter what happens to the `x` component!

### schumacher multiply matrices
    - Page 17: An example of a "phase shift":
        - Anything that changes the _optical path length_ of the
          beam, including a distance of empty space, will act as
          a phase shifter.
    - In our simplified treatment, we'll ignore the effect of
      distance and think of all phase shifters as discrete
      objects that can be either included or removed from the
      interferometer beam.

    - Page 18: look at the 'alpha' and 'beta'  matrix multiply!
    - the beta only gets multiplied by the "1" on the bottom of
      the column vector.
        - (July 2019: this was a key comment about something
          I had never understood about matrices:
            - the "numbers" refer to components _OF SOME BASIS_!)

    - Two physical situations:

        - _Situation A_: the photon is certainly in the "UPPER"
          beam and the probability amplitude for this beam is
          `1` and the amplitude for the lower beam is `0`.

        - _Situation B_: the photon is definitely in the "LOWER"
          beam. upper probability amplitude is `0`, lower is `1`.

    - The principle of superposition means these 2 situations
      impliy the existence of _many other situations_ where the
      beam amplitudes are linear combinations of the assigments
      for situations A and B.

      - Given complex coefficients `α` and `β` the
        physical situation can be written as:
        `α*(situation A) + β*(situation B)`

        - note: the complex coefficients must satisfy:
          `α^2 + β^2 = 1`

    - In the combined superposition situation,
      - amplitude for UPPER beam: ` α ∙ 1  +  β ∙ 0 = α `
      - amplitude for LOWER beam: ` α ∙ 0  +  β ∙ 1 = β `

      - Or in matrix form:
        ```
        α [ 1 ]  + β [ 0 ]   = [ α ]
          [ 0 ]      [ 1 ]     [ β ]

          [ 1  0 ] [ α ]     = [ α ]   ??? 2021: thats matrix multiplication Enda!
          [ 0  1 ] [ β ]       [ β ]   ???
        ```

        - I remember puzzling over the `[1]` for ages, not realising they were basis vectors!
                                       `[0]`

        - years later from 3blue1brown:
            - anytime we describe vectors numerically, it depends
              on an implicit choice of _basis vectors_

    - therefore the physical situation of a photon in a beam
      splitter can be summarised by a vector whose components are
      probability amplitudes.

### Stephanie gentle intro
    - Physicists discuss the symmetries of physical systems by
      imagining all possible observers who observe the same
      physical laws.
    - For a general introduction to this idea see Zee or [Gr,
      beginning of Chapter 7].
    - This is an application of what mathematicians call "group
      theory."
    - What physicists call "group theory" is a different but
      related subject that mathematicians call "representation
      theory"
    - A group is defined to be a SET with a multiplication
      operation that satisfies certain axioms...

### The discoverers: a history of mans search
    - when Galileo saw the connection between the _period_ and
      _length_ of a pendulum, he opened the way to use _time_ as
      a basis for a uniform measure of _space_
    - Dec 2017:
        - Wait! I thought the period was independent of the
          length??
    - Oct 2023:
        - Ah no: period is independent of AMPLITUDE, but is
          dependent on length!
        - Jul 2024:
            - Ah no! the period is independent of amplitude only
              for the SMALL ANGLE approximation!
                - $MY_NOTES/physics/pendulums.txt
        - Small angle approximation
            - The period  `T` of a simple pendulum (in the
              small-angle approximation) is given by the formula:
                - `T = 2 * pi * sqrt(L / g)`
            - Where:
                - `T` = the period
                        - (time for one complete swing back and
                          forth)
                - `L` = length of the pendulum
                - `g` = acceleration due to gravity.

        - From this formula, we see the period `T` is dependent
          on the length `L` of the pendulum:
            - as length increases, period increases, and vice
              versa.
            - (period is proportional to square root of length)

        - What Galileo discovered was precisely this
          relationship. He noticed the period of a pendulum was
          not significantly affected by the AMPLITUDE (i.e., how
          far back you pull it to start) but WAS determined
          primarily by its LENGTH. This was a groundbreaking
          realization.
        - The statement in the book is highlighting the
          significance of Galileo's discovery:
            - By understanding the relationship between time
              (period) and space (length of the pendulum), he
              paved the way for time to be used as a uniform
              measure for space.
            - This was crucial in developing precise timekeeping
              devices like pendulum clocks, which, in turn,
              played a vital role in various fields including
              navigation and science.

### No cloning
    - Whole argument based on _linearity_ of QM.
    - Cloning or doubling is a bit like squaring something, so
      its a quadratic operation, not linear.
    - You can clone 'ups' and 'downs', but the same machine wont
      clone 'rights' and 'lefts'.

### Mach mass Einstein resistance

- Mach felt his definition of _mass_ removed all obscurity from
  Kepler's muddled concept of 'laziness'.

- An important point about the mass concept:

    - the concept itself identifies an INTRINSIC property of
      _individual_ bodies, ie, makes possible the association of
      a unique number, the mass `m`, with each body in the
      universe.

    - HOWEVER, the phenomenon through which this is made possible
      involves _at least two bodies_ because the essence of the
      phenomenon is _mutual_ acceleration of bodies.

- This _mutuality_ of the acceleration shows that Keplers idea
  (developed by Newton) of the resistance of a body to a change
  in state is a misleading way of looking at the phenomenon, and
  is based only on subjective experience.

- When we push a body, we feel a resistance; we naturally think
  the essence of the phenomenon is resistance to pushing.
    - But this "resistance" we perceive is actually the body
      pushing us, something it must do according to the
      action/recaction law as soon as we push the body.
        - (so really the mass definition is based on Newtons 3rd
          law???)

- Thus, the essence of the mass phenomenon _is not resistance per
  se_ but MUTUALITY OF ACCELERATION.

- It's necessary to make this point because Einstein regarded an
  integral part of Mach's Principle as explaining 'resistance to
  acceleration'.

- He regarded this resistance as a resistance somehow against
  acceleration wrt the universe as a whole, with the implication
  that the resistance phenomenon would DISAPPEAR altogether if
  there were no masses in the universe.

- But this completely ignores the essence of the phenomenon,
  which is MUTUALITY OF ACCELERATION of at least TWO BODIES.

- Einstein can hardly have believed that Newton's 3rd Law (or its
  generalization in relativistic physics - (what is its
  relativistic generalisation??)) could somehow cease to hold if
  masses were progressively removed from the universe.

- And as long as the THIRD LAW CONTINUES TO HOLD, the concept of
  a quantity intrinsic to each and every body in the universe,
  the inertial mass, will survive unscathed. It must, or
  otherwise no content could be given to the Third Law.

- Jan 2019: mad the way this makes more sense at 2am at night.
  Must do more night physics.
